=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
A positive integer N is a 400 number if and only if it satisfies both of the following two conditions:

- N has exactly 2 distinct prime factors.
- For each prime factor p of N, p divides N an even number of times. More formally, the maximum non-negative integer k such that p^k divides N is even.

Process Q queries. Each query gives you an integer A, so find the largest 400 number not exceeding A. Under the constraints of this problem, a 400 number not exceeding A always exists.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
Q
\text{query}_1
\text{query}_2
\vdots
\text{query}_Q

Here, \text{query}_i is the i-th query, given in the following format:
A

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- For each query, 36 \leq A \leq 10^{12}.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5
404
36
60
1000000000000
123456789

Sample Output 1

400
36
36
1000000000000
123454321

Let us explain the first query.
There are exactly 2 prime factors of 400: 2 and 5. Also, 2 divides 400 four times and 5 divides it twice, so 400 is a 400 number. None of 401, 402, 403, and 404 is a 400 number, so the answer is 400.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

def build():
    MAXM = 10**6
    spf = [0]*(MAXM+1)
    for i in range(2, MAXM+1):
        if spf[i] == 0:
            spf[i] = i
            if i*i <= MAXM:
                step = i
                start = i*i
                for j in range(start, MAXM+1, step):
                    if spf[j] == 0:
                        spf[j] = i
    nums = []
    for m in range(2, MAXM+1):
        x = m
        cnt = 0
        while x > 1:
            p = spf[x]
            cnt += 1
            while x % p == 0:
                x //= p
        if cnt == 2:
            nums.append(m*m)
    return nums

Then queries:
data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
Q = int(data[0])
As = map(int, data[1:])
ans = []
for A in As:
    idx = bisect.bisect_right(nums, A) - 1
    ans.append(str(nums[idx]))
sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

For composites, spf[j] references the PyLong object of its smallest prime factor. So number of distinct PyLong objects stored is number of primes <=MAX, ~78k, not 1e6. for i in range(2, MAX+1):

from bisect import bisect_right

# precompute nums

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    Q = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        A = int(input())
        idx = bisect_right(nums, A) - 1
        out.append(str(nums[idx]))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

MAX_M = 10**6

def build_nums():
    spf = [0] * (MAX_M + 1)
    for i in range(2, MAX_M + 1):
        if spf[i] == 0:
            spf[i] = i
            if i * i <= MAX_M:
                for j in range(i * i, MAX_M + 1, i):
                    if spf[j] == 0:
                        spf[j] = i

    omega = [0] * (MAX_M + 1)
    nums = []
    for i in range(2, MAX_M + 1):
        p = spf[i]
        q = i // p
        if q % p == 0:
            omega[i] = omega[q]
        else:
            omega[i] = omega[q] + 1
        if omega[i] == 2:
            nums.append(i * i)
    return nums

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    Q = int(input())
    nums = build_nums()
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        A = int(input())
        idx = bisect_right(nums, A) - 1
        out.append(str(nums[idx]))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

MAX_M = 10**6

def build_nums():
    spf = [0] * (MAX_M + 1)
    for i in range(2, MAX_M + 1):
        if spf[i] == 0:
            spf[i] = i
            if i * i <= MAX_M:
                for j in range(i * i, MAX_M + 1, i):
                    if spf[j] == 0:
                        spf[j] = i

    omega = bytearray(MAX_M + 1)
    nums = []
    for i in range(2, MAX_M + 1):
        p = spf[i]
        q = i // p
        if q % p == 0:
            omega[i] = omega[q]
        else:
            omega[i] = omega[q] + 1
        if omega[i] == 2:
            nums.append(i * i)
    return nums

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    Q = int(input())
    nums = build_nums()
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        A = int(input())
        idx = bisect_right(nums, A) - 1
        out.append(str(nums[idx]))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "400 number" and "exactly 2 distinct prime factors" but "For each prime factor p of N, p divides N an even number of times" If N = p^2 q^2, sqrt=pq. If p and q are distinct primes, omega(sqrt)=2. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N has exactly 2 distinct prime factors" and
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

MAX_M = 10**6

def build_nums():
    spf = [0] * (MAX_M + 1)

    for i in range(2, MAX_M + 1):
        if spf[i] == 0:
            spf[i] = i
            if i * i <= MAX_M:
                for j in range(i * i, MAX_M + 1, i):
                    if spf[j] == 0:
                        spf[j] = i

    omega = bytearray(MAX_M + 1)
    nums = []

    for i in range(2, MAX_M + 1):
        p = spf[i]
        q = i // p

        if q % p == 0:
            omega[i] = omega[q]
        else:
            omega[i] = omega[q] + 1

        if omega[i] == 2:
            nums.append(i * i)

    return nums

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    Q = int(input())
    nums = build_nums()

    ans = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        A = int(input())
        idx = bisect_right(nums, A) - 1
        ans.append(str(nums[idx]))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
